- [Preserve valid Codex tool-context fields and keep app diagnostics tied to the latest refresh](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162032) — Thanks [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- [Reuse unchanged Codex session inspection and read current bindings outside the Gateway thread](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162037) — Thanks [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- [Preserve tabs and line breaks in Codex sandbox directory listings and recursive copies](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162610) — Thanks [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- [Prevent fleet configuration copies from exhausting Gateway memory during Codex session discovery](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162912) — Thanks [obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus), [609NFT](https://github.com/609NFT).
- [Load idle agents’ Codex session catalogs when requested instead of at Gateway startup](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163129) — Thanks [obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus), [609NFT](https://github.com/609NFT).
- [Report early workspace-write rejection as a failed Codex shell command with its original arguments](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163427) — Thanks [joshavant](https://github.com/joshavant), [100yenadmin](https://github.com/100yenadmin), [vincentkoc](https://github.com/vincentkoc).
- [Reduce repeated file checks and display preparation when browsing large Codex histories](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163706) — Thanks [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- [Stop importing older Codex sidecar bindings while retaining the files for diagnosis](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163707) — Thanks [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- [Keep linked failed patch and shell actions in saved Codex conversations for supported simple Code Mode calls](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164116) — Thanks [joshavant](https://github.com/joshavant).
- [Protect replacement Codex conversations from delayed commands and cleanup belonging to an older session](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164197) — Thanks [steipete](https://github.com/steipete), [galiniliev](https://github.com/galiniliev), [KirDE](https://github.com/KirDE).
- [Resume an interrupted Codex chat while sibling chats continue](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165487) — Thanks [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Codex startup and timeout guidance">

Codex diagnostics show more of the observed startup stage, launcher identity and pending requests when a timeout occurs. Known process-wide diagnostic-log notices stop repeating across chats.

Standalone Doctor can check selected local Linux Docker or Podman Codex runtimes for sandbox loopback failures. Run Doctor separately after an update to get this check. If managed Codex startup stops after a launcher error, repair the installation and restart the Gateway to retry; ordinary reloads do not retry that stopped startup path.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources</summary>

- [Preserve useful managed Codex startup errors when the launcher exits before its error arrives](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161419) — Thanks [steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- [Stop replaying known process-wide Codex diagnostic-log notices across chats](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161657) — Thanks [vyctorbrzezowski](https://github.com/vyctorbrzezowski).
- [Use standalone Doctor to detect local Linux Codex sandbox loopback failures](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161692) — Thanks [steipete](https://github.com/steipete), [eugifa](https://github.com/eugifa), [oc-jarvis](https://github.com/oc-jarvis), [sallyom](https://github.com/sallyom), [tedassur](https://github.com/tedassur), [Thinkscape](https://github.com/Thinkscape), [vincentkoc](https://github.com/vincentkoc).
- [Show the active phase and awaited request counts in Codex timeout diagnostics](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162927) — Thanks [vincentkoc](https://github.com/vincentkoc).
- [Show the last observed Codex startup stage and whether a client was started, joined or reused](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163156) — Thanks [vincentkoc](https://github.com/vincentkoc).
- [Distinguish Codex initialization writes, native responses and version checks in timeout diagnostics](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163230) — Thanks [vincentkoc](https://github.com/vincentkoc).
- [Identify the registered Codex launcher in startup timeout diagnostics](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163388) — Thanks [vincentkoc](https://github.com/vincentkoc).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Experimental Decision assistance">

With Decision assistance enabled and a Decision model selected, eligible built-in conversational turns can omit optional tools when the model judges them unnecessary. Required tools and permissions remain in place, and later turns start from their normal tool set. The feature is off by default, but an existing saved opt-in can activate this new automatic behavior after upgrading.

The configured provider receives bounded request text, up to two recent visible exchanges, tool-return and error counts, and resolved prompt-hook text. Hosted evaluation can incur charges, and judgments can be wrong. Disabling assistance stops new automatic evaluations; already-dispatched evaluations may still send data and their results may still restrict tools. Explicit `decision_evaluate` calls use separate controls.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources</summary>

- [Optionally omit unneeded tools for conversational turns with experimental Decision assistance](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/2f1f8beed7a3314e704d5435e90a33397b4ca787) — Thanks [MertBasar0](https://github.com/MertBasar0), [jalehman](https://github.com/jalehman).

</details>

</Accordion>

</AccordionGroup>

## Automations and Scheduling

Scheduled work is easier to find, run on demand and follow through to its result. Upgrades preserve older jobs' owners and delivery choices, while failure history and repair requests give a clearer picture of work that did not finish.

<AccordionGroup>

<Accordion title="Preserve older scheduled jobs">

Older jobs keep their historical agent and delivery settings until Doctor repairs them. When prompted, run `openclaw doctor --fix`; the normal updater's Doctor phase also handles supported repairs. Affected jobs wait for repair before running, editing or removal, and a due one-time job keeps its pending occurrence. An unknown delivery mode needs explicit review, and the editor requires a deliberate delivery choice before saving. Completely omitted delivery settings keep their normal defaults.

Restart recovery also holds interrupted one-time announcements and webhooks when delivery may already have happened. These jobs stay disabled with an **Unknown** delivery status, including jobs configured to delete themselves after running. Check the recipient before retrying, because **Unknown** can mean either sent or unsent.

**Before upgrading, make a verified SQLite backup that accounts for its write-ahead log.** Older runtimes refuse the migrated state outside the updater's temporary grace period, so rolling back the binary alone does not remove that restriction. Restoring an older backup loses newer delivery records and cannot undo messages or webhooks already sent.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Bug fixes**

- Preserve historical job owners and delivery intent through Doctor repair, with verified backups and the pending occurrence intact. Within stored delivery objects, missing or null modes and the old `deliver` spelling become `announce`; unknown modes need review. Update rehearsals retain live legacy files for the real import. [d89da759](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/d89da7595d0d6c51f4e17b5631114023e49f931b) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Hold interrupted one-time message and webhook deliveries when replay could send them again; runs proven never to have started remain recoverable. [#159873](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159873) Thanks [@joshavant](https://github.com/joshavant).
- Reject malformed stored-job saves that supply only the obsolete identity alias before changing any bundled job rows. Supported jobs and public `jobId` RPC parameters keep their existing behavior. [#163247](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163247) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Recheck current job state after another process causes an edit to be refused, allowing a later edit once that run finishes while retaining ownership checks. [0193905d](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/0193905dfa3c8c49f12d4fa432c4e20ef38d3848) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

**Background discussion**

- Earlier discussion of preserving legacy job intent [#160448](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160448).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Author jobs and choose their tools">

Scheduled agent jobs using default tool settings can use the owning conversation's current tools, including eligible older jobs with automatically captured tool lists. This can restore tools added or missed after creation without recreating the job. These defaults now follow the owning conversation's current permissions; restrictions tied only to the original sender or its plugin hooks no longer apply. Explicit finite or empty lists, scripts, condition-trigger jobs and jobs tied to app or runtime permissions keep their restrictions. Any saved execution host stays pinned.

The CLI rejects misspelled `--thinking` values before saving an agent job or accompanying edits. Use `--clear-thinking` to remove an override; a blank value does not clear it. The model picker also keeps newly discovered choices from different providers distinct, while existing saved bare model overrides stay unchanged.

Updated [recurring-job guidance](https://docs.openclaw.ai/automation/cron-jobs/payloads#authoring-recurring-jobs) helps agents put repeatable checks and bookkeeping in workspace scripts, choose exact tools and arguments, and restrict access. A script must throw to record failure; returning an object with an error field still counts as a successful script run.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Improvements**

- Give eligible default scheduled agent jobs current owner-conversation tools without rewriting saved rows. Converting one to a script or adding a condition captures a concrete tool list. Claude CLI wildcard jobs use ordinary chat behavior without a CLI tool cap. [#162432](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162432) Thanks [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus), [@jalehman](https://github.com/jalehman), [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete) and [@joshavant](https://github.com/joshavant).

**Bug fixes**

- Reject invalid nonblank CLI thinking levels before agent-job creation or editing. Recognized values still depend on model support, and previously saved invalid values need correction. [#149968](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/149968) Thanks [@Alix-007](https://github.com/Alix-007) and [@Patrick-Erichsen](https://github.com/Patrick-Erichsen).
- Preserve the selected provider when saving a newly discovered automation model, including providers that share the same model ID. Only models eligible for manual selection are offered. [425d77a8](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/425d77a86afba0d23b668081f63a26922073ee74) Thanks [@ooiuuii](https://github.com/ooiuuii) and [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).

**Documentation**

- Guide agents toward scripts for repeatable automation work, precise tool choices and explicit script failures. Script-specific advice follows whether triggers are enabled. [#160996](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160996) Thanks [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Find jobs and inspect their history">

Use `openclaw automations list --query backup` to search visible jobs by metadata such as name, description or agent. For failures across visible jobs, use `openclaw automations runs --all --status error --limit 20`. The `cron` command supports the same queries. List search covers job metadata, while run-history filters inspect execution records; `--all` on a list includes disabled jobs, and `--all` on runs selects history across jobs.

A completed message's **From** link now opens the job's History page and transcript instead of an empty temporary chat. Automation labels follow renames, and [Usage](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/usage-tracking) keeps the recorded System or human creator of earlier recurring runs when later runs begin. The [main CLI index](https://docs.openclaw.ai/cli) also lists the existing `cron show` and `cron scratch` commands, with usage in the [cron reference](https://docs.openclaw.ai/cli/cron).

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Improvements**

- Query history across visible automations with `runs --all`, retaining existing filters and pagination. Use it without a positional job ID or `--id`. [#148947](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/148947) Thanks [@Alix-007](https://github.com/Alix-007) and [@Patrick-Erichsen](https://github.com/Patrick-Erichsen).
- Search automation IDs, names, descriptions and agent names from the terminal with `list --query`; message and script contents are outside this search. [#150097](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/150097) Thanks [@Alix-007](https://github.com/Alix-007) and [@Patrick-Erichsen](https://github.com/Patrick-Erichsen).
- Follow an isolated scheduled run's message lifecycle, agent activity and available billed usage in one trace when using `diagnostics-otel`. [#161304](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161304) Thanks [@NianJiuZst](https://github.com/NianJiuZst), [@ryan-dyer-sp](https://github.com/ryan-dyer-sp) and [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Keep automation names current across history and live messages while preparing their database lookups in background workers. Deleted jobs retain the existing Automation label. [#163797](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163797) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Use less temporary memory when repeatedly listing automations or reading restart status by reusing unchanged snapshots. [#164160](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164160) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

**Bug fixes**

- Omit a stale next-run time from newly completed one-time jobs that delete themselves. An early manual run still retains its future scheduled occurrence. [#161554](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161554) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Open the durable job History page from a completed message's From link. Older runs can require manual paging, and deleted jobs cannot be opened. [#161643](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161643) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Preserve recorded creators of prior recurring runs when the next run starts, rather than moving them into Unattributed. Already-lost attribution is not reconstructed. [#161806](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161806) Thanks [@PollyBot13](https://github.com/PollyBot13), [@Conan-Scott](https://github.com/Conan-Scott) and [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).

**Documentation**

- Make the existing `cron show` and `cron scratch` commands discoverable in the main CLI index. [#145034](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/145034) Thanks [@qingminglong](https://github.com/qingminglong).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Run jobs now and keep delegated results">

When you ask an agent to run an automation, short jobs can return their actual status and summary in the same tool call. The default wait is 60 seconds, with an agent-tool limit of ten minutes. Longer jobs continue running and return a run ID for follow-up. Jobs that need the calling conversation acknowledge promptly so that conversation can finish its turn; their reports can then arrive after the initiating reply ends. Result access still follows your permissions.

Delegated jobs can return the child's answer instead of echoing their own instructions. For isolated jobs with delivery mode `none` that only delegate, the scheduler waits within the existing deadline and keeps the answer in private run history. Deliberate `NO_REPLY` remains a quiet success; a timeout or missing answer records an error and retains the transcript. These jobs can take longer and reveal failures that previously went unrecorded.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Improvements**

- Return completed short-run outcomes to the agent, and give longer runs an exact run ID for later inspection. A wait timeout leaves accepted work running; the calling agent must inspect the run later for its outcome. [#162481](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162481) Thanks [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Address an automation's stable session key to reach its latest local detached run. A newer run cancels an older in-flight follow-up; replies stay in the run transcript and do not inherit the original worktree or cloud worker. [#161085](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161085) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete) and [@ysumatta](https://github.com/ysumatta).

**Bug fixes**

- Recover the full scheduled reply when history marks its display preview as truncated. If recovery fails, the incomplete preview is not treated as the complete answer. [#160520](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160520) Thanks [@jayzhou2309](https://github.com/jayzhou2309), [@xiao398008](https://github.com/xiao398008), [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus) and [@markoub](https://github.com/markoub).
- Record and announce the child's final answer when a delegated job's parent has no substantive reply, instead of sending the job prompt. [#162460](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162460) Thanks [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Keep child results in private history for isolated, no-delivery jobs whose parent only delegates. Missing or late output follows normal error accounting, with alerts only where configured. [#162474](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162474) Thanks [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Avoid holding the caller's turn while waiting for automation work that needs that same conversation, including named main-session aliases and current-session announcements. [#162683](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162683) Thanks [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Deliver chat-triggered automation reports after the initiating turn ends, and retain affected transcript copies. [#162780](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162780) Thanks [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Retain a scheduled child's captured answer after `cleanup` is set to `delete`, while still deleting its session. The answer expires at the existing archive deadline, normally 60 minutes, or after five minutes when archiving is disabled. [#162962](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162962) Thanks [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Understand failures and repair requests">

Agents can explicitly report unsuccessful scheduled work by putting `AUTOMATION_FAILED` alone on the first line of their final reply. History records the error and its explanation, and announced explanations omit the marker. The same handling applies to adopted child answers. This is an explicit report, rather than automatic verification that the task succeeded. For a one-time job, an agent-reported failure counts as permanent even when its explanation sounds transient.

Eligible repeatedly failing jobs can ask for diagnosis and workspace repair in their owning conversation. Editing the job requires a user reply, and ordinary conversation permissions govern tools and files. A job gets one repair request per uninterrupted failure streak; its next failure sends the fallback alert, while jobs that will not run again keep their direct failure alert. Successful execution silently resets the incident. Setting `failureAlert` to `false` disables both repair and alerts.

Recurring jobs with delivery mode `none` and no active failure-alert route stay enabled through agent-reported failures, retaining error counts and backoff up to hourly. Runtime faults keep normal disablement rules, including a later runtime fault after an accumulated failure streak. A crash between saving history and job state can still disable an already-failing silent job. The [failure-notification guide](https://docs.openclaw.ai/automation/cron-jobs/delivery#failure-notifications) distinguishes alerts after repeated failures from ordinary failed-run output, which follows the job's delivery setting.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Improvements**

- Request diagnosis and workspace repair in the owning conversation for eligible repeated failures. Command jobs, stream or on-exit schedules, webhook routes and jobs without an owner keep their alert path. Repair replies can arrive outside heartbeat active hours; rejected requests fall back on the next qualifying failure. [#161007](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161007) Thanks [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Record explicit agent-reported task failures with their explanation, including for otherwise silent jobs. An unconfigured no-delivery job stays silent without alerts or repair requests. [#162391](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162391) Thanks [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).

**Bug fixes**

- Clearing a failure-alert override with `null` restores inherited policy instead of disabling alerts. Jobs previously saved with `false` still need an explicit correction. [#158869](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158869) Thanks [@masatohoshino](https://github.com/masatohoshino) and [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Show a sanitized unresolved execution warning in job details and history even when the agent turn succeeds. The warning omits raw errors and arguments; see [cron troubleshooting](https://docs.openclaw.ai/automation/cron-jobs/troubleshooting). [#158929](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158929) Thanks [@Ayushdevo](https://github.com/Ayushdevo), [@elvisquirino](https://github.com/elvisquirino), [@MertBasar0](https://github.com/MertBasar0) and [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Keep failure-alert webhooks **Unknown** when delivery is ambiguous, avoiding a fallback based on an incorrect Not delivered status. Known rejection or proven pre-send failure still permits fallback; Unknown does not confirm receipt. [#161750](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161750) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Classify adopted child failure reports correctly and retain failed-run transcripts. Deliberately silent recurring jobs without an alert route stay enabled for agent-reported failures, with normal error backoff. [#162686](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162686) Thanks [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Request repair once per failure streak even when the error changes, preserve final alerts for jobs without another scheduled attempt, and keep ordinary cooldowns after the fallback alert. [#162702](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162702) Thanks [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Suppress generic runtime try-again warnings from failed automatic repair turns while retaining authored replies and job-failure alerts. A repair resumed after Gateway restart can still emit its timeout warning. [#162772](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162772) Thanks [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Describe a failed heartbeat as an incomplete background check, with `openclaw logs --follow` troubleshooting guidance and separately disclosed diagnostic details. [#162869](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162869) Thanks [@vincentkoc](https://github.com/vincentkoc).
- Match failure wording to the selected work, so event-driven requests do not falsely appear to be failed periodic checks. Selected periodic tasks and monitor checks retain heartbeat wording. [ac85f6dc](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/ac85f6dc13ab6572f3f564a9fde920e4dd6a4d6e) Thanks [@juyterman1000](https://github.com/juyterman1000) and [@Patrick-Erichsen](https://github.com/Patrick-Erichsen).

**Documentation**

- Correct agent and user guidance about failed-run output versus threshold-based failure alerts, matching existing delivery behavior. [#162695](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162695) Thanks [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Save results and stop running jobs">

Saving job edits, results and scratch notes moves more database work away from the Gateway's main thread, so it can continue handling other events while those operations wait on storage. Saves preserve newer edits, recorded recipients and cancellation checks. Large session stores also do less work during scheduled-session cleanup, and background runs in other workspaces reuse matching prepared plugins while keeping their requested execution directories and file boundaries.

Removing a running automation now reports that cancellation was requested. That acknowledgment does not mean the work has already stopped, and cancellation cannot undo completed effects. Removing a job while a receipt check is pending blocks its next affected event, result publication or webhook.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Improvements**

- Keep Gateway events moving while completed manual or timer jobs wait to save their results, with job and execution records updated together. [#161142](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161142) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Save task edits in background workers while preserving newer edits and run markers. Confirmed commits settle without replay even if a worker reply is lost, although the caller can still see a reply-loss error. [#161241](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161241) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Save scratch notes and heartbeat proposals without blocking the Gateway thread; queued saves refuse changed jobs, revoked permissions and stale originating turns. [#161332](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161332) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Reduce periodic scheduled-session cleanup pauses in large stores by avoiding unnecessary loading of ordinary sessions' saved snapshots. [#161510](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161510) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete) and [@todddickerson](https://github.com/todddickerson).
- Further reduce scheduled-session cleanup work by selecting the relevant saved payloads before decoding them, retaining metadata checks and retention rules. [#161645](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161645) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Save results for jobs with long histories while holding the shared database writer lock for less time, and identify the operation behind slow writes more clearly. [#161655](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161655) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

**Bug fixes**

- Disclose active-run cancellation requests when removing an automation, retaining run history. An admitted run can remove its own job without cancelling itself. [#159586](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/159586) Thanks [@ericcaiwx-star](https://github.com/ericcaiwx-star), [@dh-js](https://github.com/dh-js) and [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Keep skipped-run history and startup notices tied to the agent and recipient captured at commit, including an absent default recipient. Interrupted cleanup avoids stuck local reservations and replay of uncertain writes. [#161703](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161703) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Check execution records away from the Gateway thread and stop the next affected action if its job is removed during that check. [#161935](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161935) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Avoid redundant plugin loading for Gateway background runs in other workspaces and for matching disabled or failed plugin records. Execution directories and file-access restrictions remain in place. [#164594](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164594) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Check conditions and manage scheduling lifetimes">

Scheduled conditions and scripts can query explicitly allowed MCP services. A condition returning `fire` as `false` skips its payload and the associated model call. Allow the required server tools by name or a server-specific pattern; a bare wildcard or absent allowlist enables none. Checks retain their 30-second and five-tool-call limits, including connections, and requester-scoped MCP services are unavailable. See [condition watchers](https://docs.openclaw.ai/automation/cron-jobs/schedules#event-triggers-condition-watchers) and [payloads](https://docs.openclaw.ai/automation/cron-jobs/payloads).

Manual due-only runs now honor normal retry backoff and trigger spacing. Force can bypass timing and enablement, with active-run, ownership and delivery checks still applied. Stopping scheduling sources cancels future deadlines and waits for callbacks already underway. Logbook's repeat intervals now begin after each callback finishes, so longer callbacks lengthen its cadence; Team Reports keeps its absolute due times and existing worker-stop cancellation deadline.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Improvements**

- Let conditions and script payloads query explicitly allowed MCP server tools, and skip payload execution when a condition does not fire. Unavailable servers remain absent with diagnostics. [#160995](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160995) Thanks [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Tie Logbook and Team Reports schedules to service lifetimes, waiting for admitted work before reporting resources close. Logbook uses intervals after completion, while Team Reports retains absolute due times. [#163918](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163918) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

**Bug fixes**

- Preserve stream-job status and nondecreasing loss counters when sources change or shutdown loses a worker reply. Obsolete updates cannot overwrite a replacement stream, and uncertain outcomes remain errors. [#161502](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161502) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Align manual due-only timing with scheduled retries and settle started work when a scheduling source stops. Sibling schedules continue, and elapsed stream deadlines are handled once after sleep. [#163228](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163228) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Gmail hooks and interactive Lobster workflows">

Gmail watchers retry briefly when a restart leaves their port occupied, and repeated rejected processing attempts for the same email produce fewer duplicate failure notices while retaining retries. A persistent port conflict still needs you to stop the process occupying the port and restart the Gateway or foreground watcher command.

The optional Lobster plugin can ask for feedback or structured answers in chat, pause at saved checkpoints, and continue after you correct an invalid answer. You can also cancel the remaining steps. **Protect resume tokens as secrets** — possession of a token allows resumption, and tokens are not bound to an OpenClaw user or session. See [Lobster structured input](https://docs.openclaw.ai/tools/lobster#structured-input).

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Improvements**

- Support Lobster feedback and selection checkpoints, corrected JSON or schema answers, successive pauses and explicit cancellation. The installed Lobster runtime owns saved progress and validation. [#162003](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162003) Thanks [@miguelbranco80](https://github.com/miguelbranco80), [@Patrick-Erichsen](https://github.com/Patrick-Erichsen) and [@Alix-007](https://github.com/Alix-007).

**Bug fixes**

- Retry Gmail watcher port conflicts after 5, 10 and 20 seconds for managed watchers and the foreground command, recovering from short restart overlaps. [#161503](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161503) Thanks [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus) and [@kazuyuki-eguchi](https://github.com/kazuyuki-eguchi).
- Suppress repeat Gmail admission-failure notices for the same retained email identity within five minutes, while retry attempts and diagnostics continue. The memory holds up to 1,000 identities and resets with the dispatcher, so expiry, eviction or restart can permit another notice. Failures after successful admission remain visible. [#164206](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164206) Thanks [@jayzhou2309](https://github.com/jayzhou2309), [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus) and [@TrevorDGreen33](https://github.com/TrevorDGreen33).

**Documentation**

- Point the Automations index directly to the existing [mapped-webhook instructions](https://docs.openclaw.ai/automation/cron-jobs/webhooks#mapped-hooks-post-hooks-name) for custom `POST /hooks/<name>` routes. [0ad243f9](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/0ad243f936f18008dfd307cc90fa4f7e18a44a1e) Thanks [@LinzeShi](https://github.com/LinzeShi).

</details>

</Accordion>

</AccordionGroup>

## Browser and Computer Use

<AccordionGroup>

<Accordion title="Setup and platform compatibility">

Managed browsers on Linux can find installed Playwright Chromium in the ARM64 layout and in caches redirected with `XDG_CACHE_HOME`, avoiding a manual executable-path workaround. Browser Doctor also follows each profile’s effective launch settings, giving more relevant setup advice for remote, attached and customized browsers.

Codex Computer Use readiness checks avoid an unrelated browser-authentication error on API-key-authenticated native Codex installations by checking native applications. Setup requests keep their configured timeout allowance when the system clock changes. Agents also receive the missing guidance for finding a browser window before preparation and supplying browser and page references together for actions.

The macOS app includes Peekaboo 4.7.0. Exact-window menu requests using `actionOnly` are refused when safe dispatch cannot be established; `actionFirst` is the supported fallback.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Browser setup**

- Find installed Playwright Chromium automatically on Linux ARM64. [#163281](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163281) Thanks [@jesse-merhi](https://github.com/jesse-merhi).
- Find Chromium in redirected Linux caches while preserving explicit executable and cache choices. [#163510](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163510) Thanks [@ly85206559](https://github.com/ly85206559).
- Match Browser Doctor prerequisites to effective profiles, including explicit Chrome MCP endpoints and local auto-connect choices. [#161694](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161694) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

**Computer use**

- Avoid false browser-authentication failures in native Computer Use readiness checks. [#162467](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162467) Thanks [@RomneyDa](https://github.com/RomneyDa).
- Preserve setup-request timeout allowances across system-clock changes. [#155873](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/155873) Thanks [@SunnyShu0925](https://github.com/SunnyShu0925), [@altaywtf](https://github.com/altaywtf).
- Explain window discovery, preparation prerequisites and paired browser/page references to agents. [#162286](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162286) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Update macOS desktop automation to Peekaboo 4.7.0 with its exact-window refusal and fallback behavior. [#162746](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162746) Thanks [@vincentkoc](https://github.com/vincentkoc), [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Media and network access">

Workspace-only host sessions can inspect browser screenshots, staged attachments and generated images in the Gateway media store. This grants access to the shared store, including other sessions’ media when the exact path is known. Unrelated files and links escaping approved directories remain blocked, and sandboxed tools gain no host-filesystem access.

Browser connections preserve explicit default ports when an address’s path, query or fragment contains an at-sign. The web-fetch CLI now honors `--format text` with Firecrawl through both `infer web fetch` and `capability web fetch`; some Markdown formatting can still remain. On Linux and macOS, Bun handles large computer-control screenshots and piped tool responses without the affected output failures, while existing response-size limits still apply.

If you use the retired `browser.ssrfPolicy.allowPrivateNetwork` setting, replace it with `browser.ssrfPolicy.dangerouslyAllowPrivateNetwork` to retain the intended private-network access. The old browser key is no longer honored; hostname rules and strict behavior without a grant remain in place.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Media and responses**

- Let workspace-only host media tools read the shared Gateway screenshot and attachment store. [#162285](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162285) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Honor the existing text-output option in both web-fetch command aliases. [#162036](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162036) Thanks [@LinzeShi](https://github.com/LinzeShi).
- Preserve large computer-control and MCP responses under Bun on Linux and macOS, while retaining process cleanup. [#163456](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163456) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

**Browser addresses and settings**

- Keep explicit default ports in the correct part of browser connection addresses containing an at-sign. [#161984](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161984) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Stop honoring the retired browser private-network key and use the canonical setting. [#163168](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163168) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Cleanup and diagnostics">

Automatic tab cleanup and dashboard reconciliation continue after the browser request that started them ends. They remain tied to the Browser service, which stops future sweeps and waits for active cleanup during stop or reload. Repeated unreachable-tab failures from stopped browser profiles produce fewer duplicate warnings, while retries continue and changed failure reasons can warn again. See [browser configuration](https://docs.openclaw.ai/tools/browser/configuration).

Desktop reconnects also stop multiplying cleanup attempts after a shutdown failure. **Reconnecting alone no longer retries that failed cleanup.** Check the reported process or filesystem error and perform lifecycle cleanup, such as turning Host Desktop off in Labs; a replacement can start only after cleanup succeeds. See [desktop configuration](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/config-browser-ui-desktop).

Browser rate-limit errors return without waiting for an unfinished response body to cancel. Runtime plugin inspection now identifies the registered `browser` tool. Doctor no longer detects or offers to archive old unconfigured `browser/clawd` directories, including through `doctor --fix`; those directories stay on disk and configured profiles remain supported. Sandbox browser bookkeeping uses a database worker so those writes do not occupy the Gateway’s main thread, while reservations and cleanup keep their required order.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

**Cleanup and error handling**

- Keep tab cleanup and dashboard reconciliation active for the Browser service’s lifetime. [#162312](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162312) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Reduce repeated stopped-profile unreachable-tab warnings without stopping cleanup retries. [#155211](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/155211) Thanks [@LiuwqGit](https://github.com/LiuwqGit), [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus), [@ghowkay](https://github.com/ghowkay).
- Bound failed desktop cleanup across reconnects and require explicit cleanup before replacement. [#161621](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161621) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Return browser rate-limit errors without hanging on response cancellation. [88b1a534](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/88b1a534773af8d6770de4fb52d595ca872445ce) Thanks [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Move sandbox browser reservations, activity updates and removals into the database worker while retaining cleanup ownership. [#164106](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164106) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

**Diagnostics and old profiles**

- Show the Browser tool’s name in runtime plugin inspection. [#158823](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158823) Thanks [@chopin-op60](https://github.com/chopin-op60), [@priyabrataedu-droid](https://github.com/priyabrataedu-droid), [@Spheresta](https://github.com/Spheresta), [@altaywtf](https://github.com/altaywtf).
- Retire Doctor’s detection and archival of old unconfigured browser profile residue, leaving existing directories in place. [#163709](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163709) Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

</details>

</Accordion>

</AccordionGroup>

## Plugins and Integrations

Compatible MCP Apps can now open directly beside conversations and workspace files. If you use external plugins, update affected plugins before upgrading OpenClaw; the upgrade guidance below covers retired SDK imports and older state formats.

<AccordionGroup>

<Accordion title="MCP Apps with conversations and files">

With MCP Apps enabled and a compatible server configured, you can open an advertised app without first sending a model turn, change its settings, and attach selected app content to a later message. Advertised viewers and editors can work with supported workspace files through a scoped file reference. An editor prevents stale saves when it supplies `ifMatch`; saves without it can overwrite a newer edit within the same writable-file grant. Setup skills run only when you request them.

Rich forms offer descriptions, previews, resource choices and custom entries. Native Codex forms depend on the conversation's approval policy, and calls to the same server cannot overlap within that conversation. Human questions share a ten-minute allowance per MCP tool call; ordinary work deadlines and cancellation still apply.

Messages and file opens now show their preview and Send/Open or Cancel controls inside the app pane. For repeated app-tool interactions, Control UI offers **Allow while this App is open**. That permission covers one requester, server and tool in the current view for up to ten minutes. Closing, replacing or expiring the view ends it, and reopening does not restore it. Other tools and model-initiated calls retain their own approval requirements. See [MCP Apps](https://docs.openclaw.ai/cli/mcp/apps#plugin-extensions) and [native rich forms](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/codex-harness/native-features#rich-mcp-forms).

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Changes and sources</summary>

- Open apps directly, use their settings, attach removable content, and view or edit advertised workspace-file formats. [#161747](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161747). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- File views follow appended or replaced files, consumed context clears without losing newer selections, and inactive panels explain how to restore interaction. [#164277](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164277). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- App messages, workspace file opens and confirmed board prompts use visible approval controls inside their pane. [#164493](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164493). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Repeated calls to one app tool can share a temporary approval for the current view. [#164496](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164496). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Tool and resource-template listings preserve server pagination tokens, including empty and whitespace-only tokens, to retrieve the correct next page. [#164368](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164368). Thanks [@ooiuuii](https://github.com/ooiuuii), [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- MCP session controls follow current metadata, withdrawn previews disappear, and access revoked during preparation returns an authorization error. [#163548](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163548). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Plugins adopting the conversation dock can place a chosen conversation beside their page, with drafts, attachments and removable page context. [#161957](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161957). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- On a supported host, plugins can open their own registered side panel beside the requesting conversation with `operator.write` permission. [#163428](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163428). Thanks [@jalehman](https://github.com/jalehman).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Workboard conversations and navigation">

With the optional Workboard plugin enabled, Sessions boards organize conversations by current run status, explicit rules and persistent pins. Descriptions no longer determine placement, and the earlier model-classification instructions and Refresh control are retired. A Board agent can still help reshape a view on request. Personal filters let you focus on conversations involving you or another person without changing the shared board. A board's kind is fixed at creation; deleting a Sessions board keeps its source conversations and Board agent conversation.

Cards and Sessions boards appear as nested sidebar links. A board created in Control UI also gets a top-level pin that you can move or remove; refreshing does not restore a removed pin. Large Sessions boards use prepared facts while optional pull-request details and previews arrive through background events. Board-only views need a session or PR-watcher update to refresh those facts; elapsed time or an external GitHub change alone does not refresh them.

If you roll back to an older release, Sessions boards appear as empty Cards boards and ignore placement. Cards added to those board IDs on the older release are rejected when you return to the newer version.

**Older Workboard data needs an intermediate upgrade.** If you still have Workboard KV data from before July 2026, run `doctor --fix` on OpenClaw 2026.9.7 before upgrading. Current Doctor preserves those old rows and warns about them; it does not import them. See the [Workboard guide](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/workboard) and the [older-data recovery instructions](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/d7748b561549edfe985d77216719a4ecaf3aa035/docs/plugins/workboard.md).

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Changes and sources</summary>

- Sessions boards combine current conversation status with placement rules and persistent pins, including active or failed runs without an observer health summary. [#161958](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161958), [#163761](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163761), [#163823](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163823). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Everyone, Involving me and person filters keep your board focus local to your browser and device. [#162283](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162283). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Nested board links and removable, reorderable sidebar pins make boards easier to reach. [#164604](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164604). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Sessions-board requests share prepared facts and concurrent refresh work, with missing PR details filled through background updates. [#164218](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164218). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Repeated reads of unchanged cards share server work, and committed changes invalidate the saved result. [#164301](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164301). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Saving proof, artifacts and worker logs avoids repeated card reads and database preparation. [#164120](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164120). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- A rejected attachment is deleted once, preserving the original metadata-limit error. [#162805](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162805). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Older Workboard KV records require the intermediate Doctor migration, while malformed Quick Chat widget URLs are rejected without a panic. [#163713](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163713). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Plugin upgrade requirements">

**Update affected plugins before upgrading the OpenClaw host.** Plugins importing retired whole-session-store or transcript-file APIs must use the focused replacements, sometimes with adapted calls and an explicit agent ID. Five broad channel/configuration/runtime imports, three command/Discord/Telegram facades, four Copilot token helpers and eight ready-made provider hook constants are also retired. Discord and Telegram remain supported, and ordinary Copilot users do not need to sign in again for these SDK changes. Use the [SDK migration guide](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/sdk-migration), [removed session APIs](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/sdk-migration/removed-surfaces#removed-session-and-transcript-file-apis) and [compatibility policy](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/sdk-migration/compatibility-policy) to check your plugin.

The refactored Zoom and Teams packages, and the ClickClack, Google Chat, IRC, Slack, SMS and Zalo packages using the shared secret helper, require OpenClaw 2026.9.8 or newer. Earlier compatible package versions remain an option for older hosts. That is a requirement for those packages, not a common minimum for every plugin.

Legacy plugins represented only by `openclaw.extension.json` are no longer discovered or installed by Doctor; their files remain on disk. Install the npm package explicitly and point `plugins.load.paths` at its root, or complete the repair with 2026.9.7 Doctor before upgrading. Editing bundled TypeScript in a source checkout now requires a Gateway restart; compiled installs need a rebuild first.

Historical conversation bindings without recognized plugin ownership can now refuse reassignment with a routing-conflict error. Recovery for these historical records is unresolved.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Changes and sources</summary>

- Whole-store and transcript-file SDK bridges are removed; affected plugins must adopt the supported focused APIs before the host update. [#162220](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162220). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- The `channel-lifecycle`, `channel-message`, `channel-reply-pipeline`, `config-runtime` and `infra-runtime` imports are retired, with replacement calls documented in the migration guide. [#162333](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162333). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- The `command-auth`, `discord` and `telegram-account` SDK facades are retired; use the supported channel contracts and injected runtime. [#163366](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163366). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Plugins using the retired Copilot token-exchange helpers must move to registered authentication hooks and provider-owned endpoint resolution. [#163587](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163587). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Provider plugins replace eight retired stream/replay constants with the existing family constructors; the documented retained aliases remain available. [#163591](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163591). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- The refactored Zoom and Teams plugin packages require host 2026.9.8 or newer. [#161628](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161628). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Updated ClickClack, Google Chat, IRC, Slack, SMS and Zalo packages using the shared secret helper require host 2026.9.8 or newer. [#162827](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162827). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Old extension-only npm stubs need an explicit supported installation or pre-upgrade Doctor repair. [#163231](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163231). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete), [@Dylanzhang1128](https://github.com/Dylanzhang1128), [@vincentkoc](https://github.com/vincentkoc).
- Bundled source plugins share loaded host code, so source edits take effect after restarting the Gateway. [#163639](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163639). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Supported older Codex harness plugins retain `sourceVisibleReplies`; `visibleReplies` is the canonical field and explicit message configuration takes precedence. [#163497](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163497), [#163786](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163786). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Typed approval hooks must omit the retired `requireApproval.timeoutBehavior` field; `timeoutMs` and `reason` remain supported. [#163333](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163333). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Typed configuration uses `models[ref].agentRuntime` and `agents.defaults.compaction` instead of the retired per-agent fields. [#164586](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164586). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- The newer meeting page-script factory uses `statusPrelude` and `statusCall` descriptors; older standalone builders remain available. [#163983](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163983). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Installed QA runner plugins using legacy-only registration must move their runner registration to `qa-runner-api.ts` or `.js`. [#164058](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164058). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Feishu broadcasts and agent selection accept canonical keyed rosters, while source types reject retired roster/default fields earlier. [#164103](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164103). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Fixed-proxy helper users should review zero-valued connection options. Most use Node defaults, but `maxTotalSockets: 0` fails during construction. [#161624](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161624). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Callable provider catalogs retain lifetime checks, and historical ownerless conversation bindings now refuse reassignment rather than entering the old migration flow. [#164047](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164047). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Migration-index links reach the matching replacement-API sections directly. [80f68b8862fc](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/80f68b8862fc98c843df31040af3219b6b8b99ed). Thanks [@LinzeShi](https://github.com/LinzeShi).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Plugin loading and reloads">

Model lists, passive sign-in status and session metadata remain readable while a plugin reload waits for existing work. Execution waits for the replacement where required. If the drain fails, explicitly retry `openclaw plugins reload <id> --wait` or revert the pending settings. A plugin model call that would block its own reload now reports the conflict promptly so you can retry after reload.

Native-prebuild plugins avoid repeated full-file scans, and managed npm plugins with native dependencies can load across different filesystems and supported symlinked layouts. Bun plugins retain the selected SDK host and dependency contents for their running generation.

Standalone SDK sandbox preparation refuses to provision shared state while a live Gateway or embedded run owns the same root. Run the operation inside its owning Gateway, or stop it through the service owner and wait for embedded runs to finish before an offline retry. Plugin authors handing off streamed payloads must not mutate their nested objects or buffers afterward.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Changes and sources</summary>

- Plugin reloads keep passive model, sign-in and chat metadata available through waiting and failed drains. [#162301](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162301). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Session lists, inspection and health can use the active thinking policy while a retired registry transitions to its replacement. [#162470](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162470). Thanks [@jalehman](https://github.com/jalehman).
- A plugin model call that would prevent its own reload fails promptly with instructions to retry afterward. [#162484](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162484). Thanks [@jalehman](https://github.com/jalehman).
- Ongoing sessions can use current tool-policy handlers after old plugin generations retire. [#164451](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164451). Thanks [@RomneyDa](https://github.com/RomneyDa).
- Reply model lookup honors disabled plugins for custom providers, aliases and model overrides. [120b43633fde](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/120b43633fde174329b35cb6017fad31cac635d3). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Native-prebuild loading avoids repeated full captured-file scans during inspection and startup. [#165301](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165301). Thanks [@goutamadwant](https://github.com/goutamadwant), [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete), [@exGeni](https://github.com/exGeni).
- Managed npm plugins with native dependencies load across filesystem boundaries and supported symlink layouts. For a wrong-host installation, run `doctor --fix` on the intended host, then reload. [#163876](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163876). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete), [@wlassalle724](https://github.com/wlassalle724), [@EmisvaldoSilva](https://github.com/EmisvaldoSilva).
- Bun loading follows captured dependency metadata, and its portable worker/recovery bundles include JavaScript dependencies needed by supported Node destinations. [#163052](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163052). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Bun plugins resolve valid wildcard package-import aliases with filename suffixes. [#163232](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163232). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Deferred Bun SDK imports retain the source or built host selected by their plugin generation. [#163360](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163360). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Bun retains its intended plugin loader when it exposes Node-compatible module hooks. [#163539](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163539). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Shared plugin caches follow the selected state location, and failed setup preserves its diagnostic error while attempting cleanup. [#161351](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161351). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Quarantined-tool and context-engine health reads move off the calling thread while continuing to report stored status. [#161375](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161375). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Doctor and scheduled cleanup warnings preserve both filesystem and ownership-token release failures. [#161488](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161488). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Plugins forwarding provider streams through multiple layers avoid inspecting the same event repeatedly. [#161696](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161696). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Stream and result handoff removes repeated boundary wrapping; plugin authors retain responsibility for leaving delivered payloads unchanged. [#163818](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163818). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Native hooks retry brief database locks within the existing deadline before declaring their authenticated bridge unavailable. [#161822](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161822). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Context-engine startup and reload wait for accepted health cleanup without letting an obsolete activation clear a newer failure. [#161952](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161952). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Standalone sandbox provisioning reports `GATEWAY_STATE_OWNER_REQUIRED` when the same root has a live or unverified owner. [#164203](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164203). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Remote-exec bootstrap includes required published `.setup` runtime chunks for affected external plugins such as Codex. [#165669](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165669). Thanks [@LiuwqGit](https://github.com/LiuwqGit), [@JakeBotFleet](https://github.com/JakeBotFleet).
- CLI help and command discovery release executable-owned plugin metadata resources and retain reported cleanup failures. [bfdb432570ac](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/bfdb432570aca3797c8c42230ae2488959381214). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete), [@vincentkoc](https://github.com/vincentkoc).
- Completed disposal can finish shutdown while still reporting its error; unfinished cleanup and failed prerequisites remain blocking. [cf78e6443477](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/cf78e64434775321f863c8c55369979d3ae90d07). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Saved Control UI plugin descriptors remain readable after retirement without invoking the retired plugin. [dc8393473d09](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/dc8393473d094e033eab94a66f5c51db0c6b4dc6). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Catalog-created resource controls keep their factory's plugin context, including the repaired speech-stream release callback. [a388b8f1a736](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/a388b8f1a736201531399571af7b8209569cd392). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Worker-runtime imports resolve through the registered main, worker and lifecycle package entry points. [6c31d857a79c](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/6c31d857a79c9cca42b7d8bf686d36a91851eb03). Thanks [@RomneyDa](https://github.com/RomneyDa).
- Installed-plugin ownership and conflict checks reuse resolved paths within each check. [6fc676c5b563](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/6fc676c5b563072bd29700ca0e6bb2937c195086). Thanks [@RomneyDa](https://github.com/RomneyDa).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Plugin state and background work">

Normal Gateway stops and restarts preserve plugin session state. Explicitly disabling or removing a plugin still deletes its state. Completed cleanup callbacks that report errors produce named warnings; required state failures and unfinished writes still hold the relevant shutdown barriers.

Loaded local, linked and community plugins can use their own managed stores and durable incoming-message queues. Install and loading consent still apply, as do plugin namespaces, quotas and lifetimes. Separate trust checks remain for hook-dispatched agent turns and elevated Gateway, node or channel operations. See [plugin state](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/sdk-runtime/state-and-system#api-runtime-state) and [trust troubleshooting](https://docs.openclaw.ai/tools/plugin#plugin-runtime-trust-refused).

Owner-bound scheduling stops pending callbacks when a service or account retires, invokes cleanup, then waits for admitted work before retiring shared resources. Adopted consumers include ClickClack, Device Pair, Reef, Buzz, Discord, IMAP, iMessage, Slack, Tlon and Voice Call. Standalone Voice Call keeps its webhook running after printing the call result; stop it with SIGINT or SIGTERM to complete joined cleanup.

**Retired files need a migration before this upgrade.** Device Pair notify JSON, Discord model-preference/thread-binding JSON, and iMessage reply-cache, sent-echo or catchup files require OpenClaw 2026.9.5 and Doctor. Voice Call `calls.jsonl` requires 2026.9.7. Doctor preserves the originals. Verify the migration and back up or move those retained files before retrying the upgrade.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Changes and sources</summary>

- Ordinary stop/restart preserves plugin session state and reports settled callback errors as named warnings. [#164975](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164975). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Every loaded plugin can save its own managed state and process durable incoming-message queues without the former storage trust refusal. [#165063](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/165063). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Service/account scheduling cancels pending work and joins admitted callbacks after owner cleanup, with the separate legacy-file upgrade requirements above. [7f0410c2e66e](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/7f0410c2e66e912a524e72ca883deef2dca09d0f). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Delayed session-start and session-end callbacks can finish after the triggering request closes, including `/new` rollover. [e9e1134c1809](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/e9e1134c1809dbd6f66afde81c11a06046ece012). Thanks [@xialonglee](https://github.com/xialonglee), [@vincentkoc](https://github.com/vincentkoc), [@Patrick-Erichsen](https://github.com/Patrick-Erichsen).
- Adopting session-end plugins can read an authorized bounded tail of the ended conversation during the handler lifetime; external plugins must opt into conversation access. [#161451](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161451). Thanks [@ekinnee](https://github.com/ekinnee), [@jalehman](https://github.com/jalehman), [@PaulClawitzki](https://github.com/PaulClawitzki), [@xydigitLybnnnn](https://github.com/xydigitLybnnnn), [@azuretek](https://github.com/azuretek).
- Plugin authors can await session history, extension changes and replay metadata. Transcript rewrites require awaiting both preparation and its returned commit; see [persistence migration](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/sdk-migration/how-to-migrate#await-session-transcript-persistence). [#163264](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163264). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Awaited workspace-result and publication readers are available alongside deprecated synchronous adapters that retain their return values and completion timing. [#163819](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163819). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Plugins can subscribe to changed session keys and invalidation tags, with unsubscribe and fallback reads for changes outside the subscription. [#163820](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163820). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Mention Inbox uses background storage and settles accepted records and dismissals on orderly shutdown; existing synchronous methods remain with deprecation warnings. [#164291](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164291). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Targeted Claw cleanup and MCP setup-status writes run away from the request thread, with a durable claim before cleanup effects. [#163112](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163112). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Stopping Beam and Codex desktop watchers cancels polls and retries and waits for admitted work, preventing late generation publication. [#163914](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163914). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Custom legacy PCM Talk integrations avoid starting an obsolete reply when cancellation arrives after its replacement. [#163732](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163732). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Global MCP cleanup continues reporting an earlier unconfirmed disposal instead of losing that uncertainty on a later empty cleanup. [a6089edc5cd9](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/a6089edc5cd9f53f98313b4392d3fc1566258331). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Repositories and prepared cloud workers">

Enterprise teams can configure `gateway.github.host` and `apiBaseUrl`, choose a default repository URL and optional ref, and map that project to a cloud-worker profile. New Session offers the default to people authorized to create sessions and respects their explicit project choice. Enterprise credentials must match the configured host; public GitHub sign-in remains separate, and public environment-token fallback does not authorize Enterprise service access. Configuring Enterprise repositories does not complete GitHub authentication on stock cloud workers.

Eligible connected Control UI browsers keep the profile's configured ready-worker target prepared within pool limits. Read-only viewers do not create this demand. After the last eligible browser disconnects or loses access, refill stops and unused presence reserves retire after fifteen minutes. Active sessions keep their own worker lifetime. Local recovery of an Enterprise repository needs its recorded host configuration restored.

Cloud-worker and remote-proof paths require Crabbox 0.69.0 or newer. A definitive unsupported Linux snapshot response can use the existing recovery path; older paused captures still need recovery. Qualifying coordinator timeouts can retry within the original command budget, while uncertain earlier allocation remains under the existing cleanup owner. Do not remove `--lease-id` to bypass a refusal. See [cloud-worker troubleshooting](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/cloud-workers/troubleshooting).

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Changes and sources</summary>

- Configured Enterprise repository defaults and authorized browser presence prepare matching cloud-worker capacity within the chosen pool limits. [db01f6d520b8](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/db01f6d520b844c62f146a789f3da2c11075529c). Thanks [@galiniliev](https://github.com/galiniliev).
- Crabbox 0.69.0 or newer supports recovery from definitive unsupported Linux snapshot capture without treating every capture error as a fallback. [#161465](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161465), [#162334](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162334). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- A healthy macOS Crabbox worker can re-enroll on a busy host without a false reprovisioning error. [#162233](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162233). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Crabbox cleanup stays with the selected profile, and concurrent requests share executable preparation. [#164128](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164128). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Linux profiles request Linux explicitly, and sanitized worker errors retain useful status and recent log details. [#164129](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164129). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Qualifying coordinator timeouts get at most three attempts within the original budget; fresh unsupported-backend refusals identify the profile problem. [#164231](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164231). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- GitHub publication account choices and status remain readable during conflicting retained-snapshot activity. [#161551](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161551). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Personal GitHub confirmation status follows session archive/restore and rejects reads made stale by archive changes. [#141994](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/141994). Thanks [@wangmiao0668000666](https://github.com/wangmiao0668000666), [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Agents API tools and attachments">

Agents API sessions can use configured Streamable HTTP MCP tools reachable from their execution environment, including private services. Start a fresh session with `/new` or `/reset` after changing that tool setup. Agents API also has its own installed-plugin selection; copy any needed selections explicitly rather than relying on the Codex block.

Self-hosted Agents API deployments can accept files in new or resumed turns when a registered attachment-capable workspace provider stages every file at a path the executor can read. The whole batch must be prepared before submission. Text-only turns work independently of that attachment setup. Follow the dedicated [Agents API guide](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/agentsapi).

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Changes and sources</summary>

- Fresh Agents API sessions discover and call configured Streamable HTTP MCP tools with resolved credential headers. [#160931](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160931). Thanks [@sjf-oa](https://github.com/sjf-oa), [@sjf](https://github.com/sjf).
- Agents API stores its installed-plugin selections independently of Codex, and changes to that selection block do not require a Gateway restart. [#161048](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161048). Thanks [@sjf-oa](https://github.com/sjf-oa), [@sjf](https://github.com/sjf).
- Self-hosted turns can submit staged attachments when their workspace provider makes the complete batch readable to the executor. [#161312](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161312). Thanks [@sjf-oa](https://github.com/sjf-oa), [@sjf](https://github.com/sjf).
- A retryable Agents API failure can retry after an accepted follow-up without a transcript-admission identity error; the next turn reads fresh history. [#161444](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161444). Thanks [@sjf-oa](https://github.com/sjf-oa), [@sjf](https://github.com/sjf).
- The dedicated plugin guide brings Agents API setup and capabilities together, replacing the older runtime-fragment bookmarks. [#162255](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162255). Thanks [@sjf-oa](https://github.com/sjf-oa).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Plugin authoring and discovery tools">

Tool Search finds trusted tools whose matching parameter names or descriptions sit inside combined schemas or tuple items. Repeated tool and installed-skill searches reuse the unchanged catalog index while checking current eligible descriptors. Searches still use current tool visibility and authority.

With experimental Claws enabled, `openclaw claws migrate <agent-id>` can bring an existing configured local agent under Claw management while keeping its ID and workspace. Preview the exact plan before applying it; automation uses `--dry-run --json`, then `--yes --plan-integrity <digest>`. Only supported settings and selected prompt files are adopted. Credentials, sessions, transcripts and databases stay outside that ownership, and removing an adopted Claw retains the original agent, workspace and generated package. Older readers reject the new ownership format. See [Claws](https://docs.openclaw.ai/cli/claws).

Plugin authors also gain shared meeting builders, a reusable thread-binding schema and clearer Control UI/session-reader guidance. Await the new persistence methods before dependent work; the documented synchronous adapters remain until the next SDK major, with explicit breaking-release approval also required where documented. For Mention Inbox publication callbacks, send the response synchronously and await dependent mutations afterward.

The writable meeting-caption and Logbook index upgrades can take time and temporary disk space on their first build.

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Integration setup and fixes">

Voice calls keep their recorded agent when configuration changes. Older active calls with no recorded owner are skipped; hang up through the provider and start a new call rather than expecting OpenClaw to resume them under a different agent.

Doctor repairs legacy MCP transport aliases in Gateway and node-host configuration. Run `openclaw doctor --fix` for that repair rather than relying on ordinary startup to normalize them. For supported Ollama models sent directly to the real `ollama.com` endpoint, ordinary Ollama configuration can use native maximum thinking. Local servers and local `:cloud` relays keep the compatible high mapping.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Changes and sources</summary>

- Voice Call preserves recorded ownership and leaves old unowned active calls for provider-side hangup and a fresh call. [#163118](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163118). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Doctor repairs old MCP `type` aliases, and an explicit canonical transport wins over a conflicting legacy type. [#162256](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162256). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- MCP listings preserve opaque pagination tokens, and supported models sent directly to `ollama.com` receive maximum thinking without changing local-relay compatibility. [db0c17463643](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/db0c17463643179148600aa03be5f1f0cc5815b2).
- Gmail setup keeps the full discovered Python executable path when its directories contain spaces. [fd333c7e1773](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/fd333c7e17731cfbb28375b58110b4cd30d3f52a). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
