- Malformed Claude Workshop replies reject the waiting MCP request promptly instead of leaving it until the SDK timeout. [#158916](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158916). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete), [@VACInc](https://github.com/VACInc).
- Diffs screenshots honor the readiness deadline, Canvas assets can retry after a transient read failure, and failed Twitch startup clears its deadline timer. [#161096](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161096). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Saved ClickClack discussion updates begin with service startup rather than falling back to premature polling. [#161150](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161150). Thanks [@ericcurtin](https://github.com/ericcurtin), [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

</details>

</Accordion>

</AccordionGroup>

## Security and Privacy

<AccordionGroup>

<Accordion title="Shared conversations and guest access">

Shared conversations use the selected verified participant's permissions for session actions and child work, within the permissions granted to the original run. Participants still share the conversation and its trust boundary. Guests can use private helpers, notify children they own and ask an agent to rename a conversation they created without receiving broad Gateway write access.

Team administrators can prepare roles by verified GitHub login before people sign in. Visitor invitations can also use a GitHub account without a public email address, provided the existing sign-in provider supplies the verified account ID. Listing and revoking visitor access follows verified identity across recorded email aliases. See the [user model](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/user-model), [session tools](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/session-tool) and [guest helper permissions](https://docs.openclaw.ai/tools/subagents/tool-reference).

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

- Shared turns and their children use the chosen verified participant's permissions, bounded by the original run. Model-account credentials stay the same, and permissions can be revoked during retained work. [#160525](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160525). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Guests with own-session write permission can launch hidden sandboxed helpers and receive their results, including after yielding. The helper retains the initiating person's model and sandbox restrictions. [#163367](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163367). Thanks [@shakkernerd](https://github.com/shakkernerd).
- Session-only writers can notify an authorized child they own without starting a new run. Notifications live in memory for the process lifetime, so restarting does not provide durable delivery. [#162986](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162986). Thanks [@shakkernerd](https://github.com/shakkernerd).
- Guests can ask agents to change the displayed name of a conversation they created, including under default sandbox policy. Explicit tool restrictions still apply, and renaming another created conversation requires its durable session ID. [#163421](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163421). Thanks [@shakkernerd](https://github.com/shakkernerd).
- Optional `gateway.roles.assignments.byGithubLogin` mappings use the cached verified primary GitHub login. An explicit role assignment takes priority, then the mapping, then the default; administrators can see the effective role and its source. [#163825](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163825). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Visitor invitations accept a GitHub selector without requiring a public email. Use exactly one GitHub or email selector and an existing verified Cloudflare OIDC account-ID mapping; the invitation plugin does not configure the sign-in provider. [#162666](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162666). Thanks [@shakkernerd](https://github.com/shakkernerd).
- Visitor access lists and revocation use the current verified GitHub identity to avoid matching someone else with the same handle. Explicit email selection takes priority, with exact-email fallback when identity is missing or ambiguous. [#160497](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160497). Thanks [@shakkernerd](https://github.com/shakkernerd).
- Administrators can revoke a person's recorded visitor grants across verified email aliases by profile ID, or revoke one invitation by grant ID before sign-in. This requires host support and affects the recorded grants, leaving independent access intact. [#162207](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162207). Thanks [@shakkernerd](https://github.com/shakkernerd).
- In multi-user installations, only a thread's creator or an administrator can archive or restore it. This also applies to existing threads; being assigned responsibility alone no longer grants that permission. See [multi-user conversations](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/multi-user). [#160932](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160932). Thanks [@stevenlee-oai](https://github.com/stevenlee-oai).
- Session-scoped users can read shared GitHub publication options and receipts for conversations they may view. Options require a supported current managed repository or worktree; personal accounts and publication mutations retain their own permissions. [#162984](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162984). Thanks [@shakkernerd](https://github.com/shakkernerd).
- Shared GitHub publication options remain readable when both the connection grant and role imply session read using different permission names. Later role revocation still prevents the read. [#163910](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163910). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Agent discovery follows the current role and waits for a fresh roster after reconnecting. Draft identity edits survive when they still refer to the same profile and intent, and a Stop/completion race no longer leaves a capacity slot occupied. [#161019](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161019). Thanks [@Patrick-Erichsen](https://github.com/Patrick-Erichsen).
- Sharing controls check fresh stored permissions and visibility after waiting. Outdated membership or ownership cannot carry a request into a replacement conversation, and stale cached visibility no longer turns a needed update into a no-op. [#163572](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163572). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Command and skill lists for shared conversations are withheld if access, the conversation or selected skill revisions change during discovery. [#163643](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163643). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Delayed owner, membership and suggestion changes reject outdated permissions. Cancelled or revoked suggestions cannot become new input; already accepted suggestions can settle without becoming available for replay. Provider-review pauses block new suggestions and edits while allowing dismissal and accepted settlement. [ef27c1fc2b](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/ef27c1fc2be3a68c8904e12cf6fe909f05fe4f5d). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Authorized agent work keeps its original host-issued permission object across independently loaded or reloaded runtime modules, avoiding a false permission error. Revocation, cancellation and scope limits still apply. [#163725](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163725). Thanks [@vincentkoc](https://github.com/vincentkoc).
- Private automation working notes are withheld after caller access is revoked or ownership changes. Older jobs also avoid false conflicts caused by their creation metadata. [#161387](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161387). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Approval prompts and administrative commands">

Approval links show the supplied payment, recipient, posting or automation-grant details before the decision buttons. Pending approvals also survive unrelated edits, while changes that affect the request's authority still invalidate them.

Pairing and default execution-approval commands now coordinate with the Gateway that owns their state. A newer CLI refuses these operations against an older running Gateway that lacks the required support. Update the Gateway, or stop it through its service owner and wait for ownership to release before retrying. If a mutation's result is uncertain, inspect the current requests or approval settings before retrying.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

- Standalone approval pages display supplied payment amounts and recipients, automation terms and duration, message recipients or posting destinations. This supplies decision context without granting permission. [#164534](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164534). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Approvals bound to a request survive unrelated agent and secret-store edits. Relevant permission or routing changes permanently revoke the pending approval, even if those settings are later restored. [#160856](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160856). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Commands that suppress audit output use the ordinary approval policy for both reads and writes. Eligible requests can use automatic review instead of a special human-only prompt; explicit denial, allowlists and always-ask policy remain effective. [#161718](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161718). Thanks [@jesse-merhi](https://github.com/jesse-merhi).
- Listing and revoking standing scheduled-command grants moves database administration off the Gateway main thread, with permission checks around changes. Grant validation and consumption at command launch retain their existing path. See [execution approvals](https://docs.openclaw.ai/tools/exec-approvals). [#162549](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162549). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Pairing list/approve and default approvals get/set/allowlist edits use authenticated Gateway routing or exclusive offline ownership. Refusal, missing credentials or a lost reply never triggers an automatic local replay; explicit Gateway and node targeting remains available. [#164075](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164075). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Prepared node commands with invalid file bindings or policy metadata stay rejected before execution approval. Retired top-level command-text fallbacks are removed; supported `plan.rawCommand` remains the canonical input. [#164544](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164544). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Authorized GitHub publication can include unchanged Actions workflows inherited exactly from the verified target when ancestry is unambiguous. Authored workflow edits and conflict resolutions still require full write authority. [#162396](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162396). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- A browser using a narrowed token explains insufficient permissions instead of offering a futile Retry. To recover administrator access, run `openclaw dashboard --json` on the Gateway host and open its fresh browser URL in the same browser, origin and Gateway within ten minutes. [#162951](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162951). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- A CLI `/btw` side-question execution context closes even when backend cleanup fails, so later work cannot reuse it. The cleanup error remains visible. [#162070](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162070). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Unexpected `openclaw security audit --fix` errors stop before the audit and return exit status 1. JSON callers receive the [CLI failure response](https://docs.openclaw.ai/cli#json-failures); run a separate `openclaw security audit` to inspect the current state. [#164159](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164159). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Security audits retire the model-age and provider-tier advisories `models.legacy` and `models.weak_tier`. Structured-report consumers should account for those removed IDs; the separate small-model tool-exposure check remains. [ecb897146e](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/ecb897146e5c47a6cde4e0d5552ce7d70c3700c3). Thanks [@jesse-merhi](https://github.com/jesse-merhi).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Node pairing and workspace restrictions">

Node-hosted turns follow the Gateway's workspace-only filesystem and patch-tool restrictions, including the current policy when a worker is reused. File containment uses the assigned node workspace. See [node session hosting](https://docs.openclaw.ai/nodes/session-hosting) and the [cloud-worker security model](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/cloud-workers/security-model).

An administrator can explicitly allow an agent's managed GitHub identity inside a supported Docker or Podman sandbox with `agents.entries.<id>.tools.github.allowInSandbox`. It defaults to off and requires suitable nonshared isolation. Enabling it gives sandbox code and background processes access to the full selected profile credentials, including refreshed contents. The read-only mount prevents edits, not credential use, and opting out does not revoke credentials already retained by running processes. Changed mounts require container recreation; the image still needs `gh`, allowed network access and suitable Git authentication. See the [sandbox GitHub opt-in](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/config-tools/github-identity#sandbox-opt-in).

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

- Node turns receive the current filesystem, model and patch-tool policy from the Gateway. Global and agent-specific workspace-only and `applyPatch` settings apply with session-permission precedence, and warm workers refresh their tool catalog for each turn. [10ea4739bd](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/10ea4739bd0f3d72663fd924197530bef1c4f6b6). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- The default-off per-agent GitHub opt-in supports built-in Docker and Podman backends. Missing or insecure managed credentials refuse execution, and security audit reports each opted-in agent. [#163784](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163784). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Codex host-backed file bridges that adopt the supported access contract check actual file targets for reads, opens and metadata. Denied aliases and targets changed after preparation are refused; this contract does not cover every bridge or file operation. [#150731](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/150731). Thanks [@vincentkoc](https://github.com/vincentkoc), [@eleqtrizit](https://github.com/eleqtrizit).
- New uploads reach reused writable SSH sandboxes, with managed originals and preprocessing or preview files retained for restart. Read-only sandboxes continue to reject writes; this covers inbound uploads. [#158026](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/158026). Thanks [@Olli0103](https://github.com/Olli0103), [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus), [@DaveFan-NCHC](https://github.com/DaveFan-NCHC), [@FelixIsaac](https://github.com/FelixIsaac).
- Strict `OPENCLAW_FS_SAFE_NATIVE_MODE=require` uses stronger native checks for file mutations. Recursive cleanup can refuse on Windows or Linux without `openat2`, leaving directories behind; use a supported platform when those guarantees are required. Default automatic mode remains best effort. [#160617](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160617). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Filesystem upgrades preserve legacy `require` settings instead of silently relaxing them and add the Windows dangling-symlink exclusive-create safeguard. Move `FS_SAFE_PYTHON_MODE` and `OPENCLAW_FS_SAFE_PYTHON_MODE` to their `*_NATIVE_MODE` names. Remove `FS_SAFE_PYTHON`, `OPENCLAW_FS_SAFE_PYTHON`, `OPENCLAW_PINNED_PYTHON` and `OPENCLAW_PINNED_WRITE_PYTHON`. Deprecated Plugin SDK hints remain accepted but ignored; direct fs-safe consumers must use `configureFsSafeNative` and `FsSafeNativeConfig` without `pythonPath`. [#163408](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163408). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Windows plugin paths reject foreign shares and ambiguous device paths before inspection. Use an admitted relative path or the root's host/share spelling for a rejected alias; removing namespace prefixes is not a safe repair. [#162574](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162574). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Agents API hosted VMs can use enabled, disabled or restricted networking with 1–100 exact hosts. Subdomains and redirects need their own entries, and adding, changing or removing the policy requires a session reset. Hosted stdio MCP requires enabled networking. See [hosted network controls](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/agents-api/environments/openai-hosted#control-network-access). [#161004](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161004). Thanks [@sjf-oa](https://github.com/sjf-oa), [@sjf](https://github.com/sjf).
- An already-paired local native app can add its first node role with the same key under the existing local auto-approval policy, including an eligible pending retry. Token replacement, revocation, remote and browser connections retain approval requirements. See [pairing](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/pairing). [#163730](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163730). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Eligible silently paired same-machine native nodes can receive their initial permitted capabilities without an extra review panel, including an app in remote mode whose actual connection is local. `autoApproveLocal=false` retains manual approval; later capability additions, command denies, OS permissions and execution approvals still apply. [#163862](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163862). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Secret storage and setup">

Credential prompts wait for a confirmed save before reporting success. Reset can hide an in-flight question immediately while its accepted save finishes privately, without changing a replacement question. If the save succeeds but runtime refresh fails, use `secrets.reload` instead of submitting the answer again.

Older core secret-reference objects that contain a source and ID but no provider require `openclaw doctor --fix`. Configuration credentials use the configured provider for that source; auth profiles retain their historical default. Originals remain in the existing config backups, auth backups or source archives. Talk and realtime Talk need particular care because their setup path may discard an older providerless API-key object before Doctor repairs it. Preserve the originals and inspect the repaired configuration before using it.

Logging out a still-saved model profile also removes its copied credentials from generated and retained catalogs. Secret-excluded Git backups omit catalog API keys and headers. Previously created or pushed backups remain unchanged, so review older backups separately.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

- Secret-setting operations run through database workers and wait for acknowledged persistence. Credential questions publish only the safe stored marker; accepted saves survive reset, and compensating rollback cannot overwrite a newer writer's entry. [31806d84f3](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/31806d84f3bb35b4389fe06ed3963734d01855cf). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Core credential readers require explicit `source`, `provider` and `id`. Doctor repairs registered legacy paths and reports removed extra fields; public SDK input adapters still accept plain providerless references, while extended providerless auth inputs reject before saving. Keep extra metadata outside reference objects. [2c0278b46c](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/2c0278b46cd29614fc08f65a1632f4f863146f4e). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Profile logout scrubs matching copied catalog credentials while the profile is still saved, and prevents in-flight work from restoring them. Profiles already removed by an older logout cannot be retroactively cleaned this way; an incomplete guarded restoration needs profile inspection. [#143647](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/143647). Thanks [@jason-allen-oneal](https://github.com/jason-allen-oneal), [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus), [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete), [@NovaUnboundAi](https://github.com/NovaUnboundAi), [@CHAESH](https://github.com/CHAESH), [@matthewmoroz](https://github.com/matthewmoroz), [@sunhsiao](https://github.com/sunhsiao), [@ndakota79](https://github.com/ndakota79).
- Agent command environments and configured secret values are read in a worker instead of querying on the Gateway main thread. Commands reuse one snapshot per run and refresh next run; cancellation prevents launch without poisoning later reuse. Snapshots over 32 MiB fail visibly instead of supplying a partial environment, and individual values retain the 64 KiB limit. [#164242](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164242). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Permitted CLI commands work with the secret egress proxy by using the admitted run context. Existing approvals and retired-run restrictions continue to apply. [#160760](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160760). Thanks [@VACInc](https://github.com/VACInc).
- The secret egress proxy can forward permitted HTTP requests to literal `localhost`, `127.0.0.1` and `[::1]` addresses. DNS aliases are excluded, and secret substitution remains refused over HTTP. See [secret storage and egress](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/secrets/secret-store-and-egress). [#160974](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160974). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- LM Studio loading and Copilot discovery or embedding errors mask credentials reflected from encoded request headers. Chutes also accepts the IPv6 loopback OAuth callback `[::1]` while retaining the redirect URI. [#162488](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162488). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Multi-agent OAuth synchronization visits the shared credential owner before sibling agents, preserving inheritance without unnecessary new credential copies. Previously duplicated stored credentials are not removed by this fix. [#163243](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163243). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- The secrets index opens the existing [Bitwarden Secrets Manager](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/secrets/integration-examples#bitwarden-secrets-manager-bws) and [password-store](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/secrets/integration-examples#password-store-pass) recipes at their correct sections. [721efcefc0](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/721efcefc0c79af2ec7e2af21c47f283e8ee6601). Thanks [@Shenrui-Ma](https://github.com/Shenrui-Ma).
- A2A status identifies peers with unresolved token settings and tells operators what to repair before reload or restart. Automatic plugin setup retains credential-resolution facts through configuration rewrites, and adding another peer preserves unavailable peers in saved configuration. Deliberately tokenless outbound peers retain anonymous sending. [cb24ec1fa7](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/cb24ec1fa7d5ce1d7162eedec3dc9447d391e6a8). Thanks [@eleqtrizit](https://github.com/eleqtrizit).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Secret masking and saved context">

Secret masking preserves ordinary text after `pass:` and avoids stalls on affected long token-like strings. Custom patterns again scan bounded chunks, so patterns that cross a chunk boundary can still miss. Diagnostic fallback output also applies the existing secret recognizers when normal formatting fails.

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

- Ordinary prose following `pass:` remains readable, while supported password records and affected long-input Bun cases keep their default masking. Previously lost words in stored output are not restored. [#160888](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160888). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Built-in token masking handles affected long strings containing plus signs, slashes or equals signs without the previous stalls. [#161089](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161089). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Custom `logging.redactPatterns` use 16 KiB chunks again. Matches spanning chunks can be missed, and anchored patterns apply at each chunk's start; built-in data-URL handling remains separate. [#161514](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161514). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- WebSocket log values use secret masking and length limits when JSON serialization fails. Invalid log-level warnings also mask values recognized by the existing redactors before writing to stderr. [#164177](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164177). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Secret masking reuses compiled matchers to reduce temporary regular-expression allocations while keeping the same masked output. [#164152](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164152). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Cleanup of old generated-HTML trust records preserves a newer record written for the same path while filesystem inspection waits. Existing exact-byte trust and path restrictions continue to apply. [#164424](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164424). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Reserved private runtime-context entries are excluded when compaction summaries and saved CLI notes read existing transcripts, including entries lacking replay metadata or opting out of replay. Stored transcript bytes are not rewritten. [#164561](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164561). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Dependencies and private-network settings">

OpenClaw-owned Bun launches stop automatically downloading missing dependencies, and the macOS app's bundled runtime disables implicit installation by default on Apple Silicon and Intel. Install required dependencies explicitly. Existing service definitions gain the launch flag through normal reinstall or update; direct stock-Bun launches should use `--no-install`. See [Bun installation](https://docs.openclaw.ai/install/bun).

Private-network Matrix, Mattermost and Tlon servers require the nested `network.dangerouslyAllowPrivateNetwork` opt-in. Repair old Matrix and Mattermost flat-key settings with Doctor on 2026.9.7 before upgrading. Tlon retains Doctor migration during updates; direct binary replacement needs `openclaw doctor --fix`. Follow the [configuration migration guide](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/doctor/config-migrations).

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

- Managed Bun services and OpenClaw-owned subprocesses fail locally on missing imports instead of installing packages at runtime. User commands, `bunx`, arbitrary third-party launchers and worker-thread resolution remain outside this launch policy. [#163270](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163270). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- The bundled macOS runtime disables implicit package installation across helpers and children using that executable. Explicit installation controls remain available. Its dependency update also reduces duplicate file-watch events and fixes queued WebSocket upgrades, event-listener exception propagation, process-title visibility and worker heap reporting. [#163313](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163313). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Matrix, Mattermost and Tlon use the supported nested private-network setting; an old flat key no longer grants access or defeats it. Matrix still permits access when either the root or account-level canonical opt-in is true, so account-level false does not override root-level true. [#163535](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163535). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Project `.env` files no longer control the eight Homebrew download, source and configuration variables involved in installation. Move needed overrides to the launching shell or global environment; ordinary project preferences remain supported. [#150912](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/150912). Thanks [@mmaps](https://github.com/mmaps).
- Docker builds from a source checkout exclude root and nested `.crabbox` directories, keeping their transient credentials and per-run state out of build images. [#164163](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164163). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- The locked dependency graph uses patched `source-map-js` 1.2.2 for the indexed-source-map denial-of-service advisory GHSA-68fv-2mgg-jv7q. [6d86072f8c](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/6d86072f8c4cede7419d9b53f84dd85430c33ce4). Thanks [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete), with review by [@shakkernerd](https://github.com/shakkernerd).

</details>

</Accordion>

</AccordionGroup>

## Quality-of-Life Improvements

Code Mode can retain small working values across replies, and required calls can stay pending until their results are collected. Related changes improve child follow-ups, session controls, workspace preparation and everyday recovery guidance.

<AccordionGroup>

<Accordion title="Keep Code Mode working data across replies">

Interactive Code Mode can save small JSON values with asynchronous `store` and `load`, then reuse them in later cells, follow-up replies and Gateway restarts. This works in session-bound Node and QuickJS cells and saves explicit data rather than variables, functions or suspended programs. Only completed cells commit, with a limit of 256 KiB per value and 1 MiB of live values. Headless, `restartSafe` and unbound cells are unsupported. If persistence reports a warning, verify the transcript before assuming the value was saved.

**Deleting or overwriting a value leaves older versions in transcript history and exports.** The live-value limit does not cap accumulated history. See the [session store guide](https://docs.openclaw.ai/tools/code-mode/guest-api#session-store).

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

- Save small JSON working values in interactive Code Mode and reuse them across cells, replies and Gateway restarts. Only completed cells commit; values are limited to 256 KiB each and 1 MiB live total, with nonempty keys up to 256 characters. Headless, `restartSafe` and unbound cells are unsupported. Deleted or overwritten values remain in transcript history and exports. [#164086](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164086). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Track reusable Code Mode workers with their Gateway or CLI host and wait for hosted workers on shutdown. Another live host keeps its own pool; the shutdown ordering takes effect after restart. [#162141](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162141). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Collect required results and continue delegated work">

An agent can mark OpenClaw shell commands, Code Mode programs and child-result collectors with `awaitResults:true` to keep the call pending until needed results are collected, including failed results. Code Mode resumes the same program from completion events without asking the model to poll. Deadlines and the original execution allowance still apply; only waiting for tools outside the JavaScript engine pauses that allowance. Required shell calls cannot use `background:true`, and required collectors cannot set `timeoutSeconds`. Intentionally detached services keep their existing behavior. If your calls use the earlier `required` argument, change it to `awaitResults`; the old key is ignored.

Child results also survive more overlapping completions and follow-ups. A paused child still needs an authorized sender to continue it. **`sessions_send` now returns one peer reply, without an automatic reciprocal exchange or target-channel post.** Send again to continue, and use `message` with an explicit channel and target to post to people. `ANNOUNCE_SKIP` and `REPLY_SKIP` are ordinary reply text. See [session-tool guidance](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/session-tool).

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

- Add completion waiting to OpenClaw shell `exec`, Code Mode `exec` and `agents_wait` so needed results remain pending until collected. The argument introduced as `required` is now named `awaitResults`. Code Mode resumes the same program from completion events; required shell calls cannot be detached, and required collectors cannot set `timeoutSeconds`. [#162707](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162707). Thanks to [@VACInc](https://github.com/VACInc), [@SparcleAI](https://github.com/SparcleAI), [@jalehman](https://github.com/jalehman).
- Avoid a stale command-completion message after a visible child agent has already finished. [#147432](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/147432). Thanks to [@Tosko4](https://github.com/Tosko4), [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus), [@VACInc](https://github.com/VACInc).
- Guide ordinary agents to handle routine work directly and delegate when independent work has a clear benefit. [#151599](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/151599). Thanks to [@jalehman](https://github.com/jalehman).
- Deliver the parent’s answer after private child-agent waiting, following the conversation’s reply policy while keeping child findings private. [#156540](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/156540). Thanks to [@obviyus](https://github.com/obviyus).
- Recover a parent continuation after supported failures publishing a completed child result, without repeating the saved change or result. Recovery waits if its original queued intent cannot be verified. [#160472](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160472). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Move initial child-agent waiting, result handoffs and saved-agent restoration into background database workers, preserving the original caller and interrupted handoff progress. [#160778](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160778). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Read less saved-session data for child recovery checks. Guarded Browser cleanup requests an update and leaves tabs untouched when its installed artifact lacks the required cleanup contract. [#160831](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160831). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Preserve a resumed child agent’s finite or unlimited time allowance within the same session, and give restart-recovered work the ordinary agent budget. Older registrations without session identity use ordinary policy, including its 48-hour default unless it is also unlimited. [#161078](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161078). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Notify a parent once when a completion-announcing child explicitly pauses for a message, so the parent can send continuation and still receive the final result. Notices do not automatically resume work or backfill already-paused records; foreign or reset requesters are rejected before input is saved. [#161114](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161114). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Let parents wait for accepted watched follow-ups to existing children, including queued work, and retain separate answers across parent-turn retirement. If accepted steering returns sentBeforeError, inspect the target before retrying. [#161130](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161130). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Keep child-completion bookkeeping from leaving the parent stuck waiting or interrupting its resumed work, including after a Gateway restart. Plugin execution remains independent of parent delivery. [#161194](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161194). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Preserve a waiting agent’s handoff through tool cleanup so it can resume without a premature ended-turn error. [#161197](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161197). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Allow a valid follow-up to a finished child while its completion record is still being saved, using a bounded additional wait while still rejecting retired callers and replaced runs. [#161279](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161279). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Earlier correction to successful no-announcement child replies, superseded by the single-reply delivery change in this release. [#161542](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161542). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Keep a finished private child’s answer available when its parent checks status before yielding. [#161820](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161820). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Return one peer reply from `sessions_send`, replacing automatic reciprocal exchanges and target-channel posts. Send again to continue, or use `message` with an explicit channel and target to post to people. `ANNOUNCE_SKIP` and `REPLY_SKIP` become ordinary text; use the required `message` field and `delivery.status` instead of the removed aliases and `delivery.mode`. [#162227](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162227). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Record a failed or timed-out child’s explanation in its own transcript when it has not produced an assistant reply, even after the requester finishes. [#162297](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162297). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Retain accepted reply authority before a nonblocking session send returns, so eligible peer and child answers can arrive after the requester ends its original turn. Nonblocking waits only for handoff; access revocation, Gateway replacement and lost ownership still stop delivery. [#162299](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162299). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Show the normal waiting acknowledgment for an accepted child message wait instead of a false missing-continuation warning. [#162306](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162306). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Include a successful child with an empty final reply in the parent’s result batch, retaining its task identity and explicit no-output result without reviving old text or intentionally silent announcements. [#162542](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162542). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Keep delegated follow-ups owned until their answer is processed and recover missing answers without repeating completed tool effects. Doctor removes retired silentReply.internal settings, and worker upload cancellation no longer produces an unhandled file-opening error. [#162567](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162567). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Preserve the parent’s wakeup when sibling child tasks finish together, and prevent delayed result reads from overwriting newer retry timing or counts. [#162760](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162760). Thanks to [@VACInc](https://github.com/VACInc).
- Keep compact subagent lists consistent with full registry reads when saved metadata repeats a JSON key. [#163135](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163135). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Preserve parent wakeups when child completions overlap yields, pauses, cancellation or recovery. [#163195](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163195). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Use recorded child ownership to select the right agent’s task for follow-ups, steering and cancellation when session keys overlap. Older ownerless rows still use key-only matching. [#163282](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163282). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Let finished-child cleanup notify its context engine after the initiating request ends, and keep cleanup tracked through shutdown with readable redacted errors. [#163331](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163331). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Preserve a child’s completed first result when an ordinary follow-up starts before parent delivery, giving each task its own result and delivery receipt. Keep revoked-turn checks and protect the newer child session from old execution cleanup; paused continuation and steering retain replacement behavior. [#163352](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163352). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Deliver required delegated results when silent sibling tasks share the batch. Silent-only tasks can finish cleanup without starting a parent turn, while cancellation identifiers and completion ownership checks remain intact. [#163677](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163677). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Avoid a false browser-cleanup failure when another completion callback or newer child run owns cleanup. [#163741](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163741). Thanks to [@vincentkoc](https://github.com/vincentkoc).
- Suppress obsolete pause notices after a quick default child follow-up; later follow-up adoption preserves eligible replies for the original requester. [#163814](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/163814). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Deliver an eligible still-running default plugin follow-up once when it was accepted just before a child pause. Follow-ups completed before the pause and restart before adoption remain outside this repair. [#164101](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164101). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Let active registered visible and hidden child agents wait for an incoming message with sessions_yield. Reject missing, stopped or superseded tasks honestly; an authorized sender must still provide the continuation and owned process results must still be collected. [#164463](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164463). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Allow queued shared-compute work to reach another eligible cooperative checkpoint instead of repeatedly targeting a worker already waiting for a host response. [#164537](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/164537). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Accept a completed child’s follow-up while browser cleanup is pending, keeping old cleanup from taking over the replacement. [b725cc7](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/b725cc7b27f64ae1b1cf72eccc0bd5959d982839). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Deliver completed child results when unrelated bookkeeping changes during delivery preparation, while rejecting genuinely replaced replies. [dab6c79](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/commit/dab6c7975c1969fbcf13d6614ba9a0ff00dca556). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

</details>

</Accordion>

<Accordion title="Stop children and recover interrupted replies">

Stopping children now preserves more completed cancellation work through concurrent session updates and reports later cleanup errors. Swarm Stop waits for the selected work and its cleanup, which can remain pending after a terminal status appears. After a timeout or incomplete result, inspect remaining children with `subagents action=list` and retry; replacement work needs its own Stop request. If child registration fails after launch, the error retains the child session and run IDs, and capacity stays reserved while cleanup is pending. Inspect that child before retrying.

Interrupted conversations retain completed tool results, and silent background-command timeouts can wake their original conversation when automatic completion wake is enabled. Check external effects before repeating non-idempotent work. See [child operations](https://docs.openclaw.ai/tools/subagents/operations) and [background-process handling](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/background-process).

- Explain when recent messages exceed the context limit and suggest a new session with a brief summary. [#160934](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/160934). Thanks to [@RomneyDa](https://github.com/RomneyDa).
- Keep completed background tool results available for final answers and empty-answer retries, and keep internal finalization prompts out of user-message history. [#161091](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161091). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Finish stopping a child during an overlapping session metadata update, preserving both the update and recorded stop across retry or restart. <Accordion title="Keep conversation controls with the right session">

Session controls better preserve other agents’ work when short session keys overlap, and simultaneous first sessions for a new agent can share database creation. A clarification sent mid-turn keeps the first executable tool or parallel batch; remaining unstarted sequential calls can still be skipped. Use `/stop` or `/queue interrupt` to cancel. Replies suppress literal internal status blocks and keep each steering message’s context together. Cloud-worker context containing unsupported media explicitly asks you to stop or reclaim the session and retry locally.

You can also import reachable native-tool conversation text into an independent OpenClaw session. This preserves projected text rather than raw native files, native-tool resumption or continuous backup; large imports report when the copy is incomplete. See [session controls](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/session), [steering](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/queue-steering) and [transcript handling](https://docs.openclaw.ai/reference/transcript-hygiene).

<details class="release-source-toggle">
<summary>Sources and complete change list</summary>

- Assign responsibility for a visible shared session to a registered person or configured agent. Access and credentials keep their existing limits; “me” requires an administrator-attested profile link. [#154239](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/154239). Thanks to [@VACInc](https://github.com/VACInc), [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Keep run files and completion records attached to their original agent and conversation after active run context clears. [#161119](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161119). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Share a pending local identity lookup across simultaneous Codex and Session Share discovery requests, reducing duplicate startup database work without changing discovered nodes and sessions. Missing or failed identity reads remain retryable. [#161496](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161496). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Avoid duplicate validation when Gateway clients establish warm message subscriptions, while preserving access checks and approval replay. [#161555](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161555). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Preserve another agent’s active reply, queued messages and commands when stopping, deleting, resetting or archiving a conversation with a colliding session key. The selected agent’s own work is still cancelled. [#161736](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161736). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Import reachable native-tool conversation text through the Control UI or `sessions import` CLI. Repeat imports append unseen items for the same agent and full source locator. New copies are creator/admin-visible drafts when drafts are enabled, otherwise they follow Gateway defaults. Copies keep at most the newest 50,000 items or 64 MiB of projected text. Explicit versus implicit home locators can create separate copies, and items without stable IDs can be skipped if identical to older text after the import window fills. [#161955](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/161955). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).
- Preserve other agents’ queued replies and commands during review pauses, workspace placement changes, rewinds, broad aborts and interrupts sharing a short session key. Broad cleanup removes only the selected agent’s own work across session incarnations. [#162179](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/162179). Thanks to [@steipete](https://github.com/steipete).

- Release a deleted agent’s model, authentication, memory and database resources without restarting the Gateway.
